# radiance kernel libraries

Generated by `rad-kbench`. Every kernel of every loaded library ran on the same inputs and each
case that passed was timed in the same run, so correctness, completeness and speed come from one run
(the Speed section says how the times were taken).

- fixture: `data/kernels.rkb`, 741 case(s) recorded 2026-10-15 from `qwen3.8-27b-fp8.rad`
- libraries: `libavx@0.1.0 libr4d@0.5.0 libref@0.1.0`
- reference: `libref`; every correctness number is relative to it, so it has none of its own
- 2202 case(s) checked, 2202 passed, 0 failed, 7 skipped, 0 capped; worst rel_l2 4.270e-03
- 734 reference result(s) taken from the cache; every comparison ran

## Libraries

`ops` is how many of the 147-op vocabulary this library has a row for, and `graph` how many of
the 43 ops this container's declared graph asks for.

`rows` is how many kernel rows the library declares and `run` how many of them a case in this
pass reached; the Rows nothing ran section lists the rest.

| library | version | domain | rows | run | ops | graph | cases | pass | fail | worst rel_l2 | median us |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `libavx` | 0.1.0 | host | 100 | 42 of 100 | 100 of 147 | 43 of 43 | 734 | 734 | 0 | 9.01e-04 | 78.91 |
| `libr4d` | 0.5.0 | device | 291 | 48 of 291 | 142 of 147 | 43 of 43 | 734 | 734 | 0 | 4.27e-03 | 12.78 |
| `libref` (oracle) | 0.1.0 | host | 123 | 42 of 123 | 123 of 147 | 43 of 43 | 734 | 734 | 0 | 0.00e+00 | - |

Rows declaring an optional hook (tuning, fusion, weight layout):

| library | tunable rows | fusion claims | weight layouts |
|---|---|---|---|
| `libavx` | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| `libr4d` | 84 | 17 | 35 |
| `libref` | 0 | 0 | 0 |

## Speed

How the times were taken (`--bench`, tools/kbench_timer.h):

- **Device rows** are device timestamps around a batch of back-to-back launches, divided by the
  launch count. The batch is queued behind a gate the host opens once all of it is queued,
  so neither the host's submission rate nor its synchronisation is in the interval, and a
  keep-alive copy runs ahead of the gate so the card does not idle (and drop its clock) while
  the host submits. Correctness ran first; the passing cases were then timed back to back after
  one sustained warm-up, two untimed launches ahead of every batch. A batch lasts at least a
  millisecond and fifty launch floors.
- A time **includes the launch floor** below, the gap between two dependent dispatches, which
  the engine pays too; a kernel near the floor is launch-bound, not slow.
- In-place operands are restored from a pristine copy before every launch, so mutation does not
  drift. The restores are timed in a batch of their own, each followed by a floor launch in the
  kernel's place, and subtracted, the floor added back (`restore` in the notes is their share
  of the batch). A large share leaves the kernel a small difference of two large
  times, which the interval shows as noise but not as bias (the restores leave the operand in
  the cache): a share of 75% or more, or a time under the launch floor, is noted as an order
  of magnitude.
- The timing pass draws its own **routing and block tables**: expert offsets and ids skewed as
  real routing is (Zipf, exponent 0.5) and block tables as permutations. The checked launch
  keeps the recorded draw.
- `±%` is the half-width of a distribution-free 95% interval for the median, as a percent of
  it (below six batches the range, which covers less); a case is timed until it is 1% or
  the rep cap (`--bench-reps`) is reached, so a wide one marks a noisy case. `n` counts the
  timed batches. `MHz` is the firmware's averaged graphics
  clock read after each batch; a batch during which a throttler came on that was off at idle and
  after the warm-up is discarded and repeated.
- **Host rows** (their own column) are steady_clock times on the calling thread, after an untimed
  launch, with in-place operands restored outside the timed region; weights, weight tables and
  inputs of 8.00 MiB or more are rotated over copies spanning 256.00 MiB (twice the CPU's last-level
  cache, at least 256 MiB), so read from memory. The notes name the OpenMP threads.
- `operands` is the total the case allocated, not what the kernel read (a gather touches M rows
  of a table, not the table), so dividing the two gives no bandwidth figure.

Device timing and its calibration:

> device: AMD Radeon AI PRO R9700 (gfx1201), the aql backend
>
> cold: each launch reads its weights, weight tables and inputs of 8.00 MiB or more from one of a set of copies spanning 256.00 MiB, so from memory; smaller inputs stay warm, as the previous op leaves them; in-place operands are restored before every launch and the restores' own time subtracted
>
> launch floor: 2.14 us a launch back to back in a batch, 4.84 us alone between two events (the backend's own 4-byte fill, the cheapest kernel a tool can launch); a batch lasts at least 1000 us
>
> read bandwidth (ranged copies into a 1 MiB ring, so the writes stay in the cache), warm / cold: 4.00 MiB 320 / 324 GB/s; 16.00 MiB 1417 / 480 GB/s; 64.00 MiB 1764 / 602 GB/s; 256.00 MiB 629 / 629 GB/s
>
> clock: 3245 MHz averaged graphics clock after a 2000 ms warm-up (gpu_metrics); on at idle, not counted: TEMP_HOTSPOT; on under the warm-up's load, binding throughout and not counted: PPT0; a batch during which another throttler came on is repeated

| op | kernel | library | geometry | device us | host us | min | p90 | n | ±% | MHz | operands | notes |

## Memory safety

Every device allocation in this run (operands and kernel scratch) had a poisoned 256-byte margin
on both sides, verified after the checked launch. This catches out-of-bounds writes, which
corrupt a neighbouring tensor; out-of-bounds reads go unseen.

AMD's amdgcn AddressSanitizer (`/opt/rocm/amdgcn/bitcode/asanrtl.bc`) needs `xnack+` for its
page-fault-based shadow, which RDNA does not implement (`rocminfo` reports `XNACK enabled: NO`),
so the margins stand in for it.

- 734 case(s) ran inside guarded allocations
- 0 wrote outside one

## Not covered

A skip means the tool declined a case rather than invent an extent, a dtype or an oracle it
does not have. Each line below is a gap in coverage, not a failing kernel.

**Collective ops are not checked.** `all_reduce`, `all_gather` and the fused all-reduce forms
rendezvous with peer ranks over peer memory; in a single process a kernel waiting for a peer
hangs. They appear in the completeness matrix but carry no correctness or speed number.

| what | cases |
|---|---|
| logit_rerank  (one case needs 14.21 GiB, over the 8.00 GiB budget -- raise --max-bytes to check it) | 7 |

## Static and dynamic analysis

Advisory, and generated separately from the measurements above:
`tools/kbench-analysis.sh` runs on a machine that need not have a GPU.

### clang-tidy

`bugprone-*`, `clang-analyzer-*` and `performance-*` over every host translation unit
(`libref/`, `core/`, `tools/`). HIP units are excluded: clang-tidy cannot replay the
offload flags and reports `__device__` as an error rather than analysing the body.
